STAGE 03.1 IMPLEMENTATION GUIDE
Add Historical Continuous Maize Suitability (~1 km) to the Existing Parcel A Stage 03 Workflow
===============================================================================================

DOCUMENT PURPOSE
----------------
This document instructs a coding agent to extend the existing, successfully executed Parcel A Stage 03 workflow without deleting, simplifying, replacing, or silently changing any existing functionality.

The current project remains focused only on maize. The new component is the historical continuous maize suitability index from GAEZ v5, mapset RES05-SXX30AS, for the historical period 2001-2020.

The coding agent must preserve the complete existing Stage 01, Stage 02, and Stage 03 code and outputs. This is an additive Stage 03.1 extension. It is not a redesign of Stage 03 and is not permission to remove any previous maps, tables, graphs, metadata, QA checks, source records, legends, downloads, or dashboard controls.

CURRENT MAIZE ROADMAP
---------------------
Completed before this extension:
- Agro-ecological context.
- Historical length of growing period (LGP).
- Soil quality.
- Most limiting soil quality.
- Irrigated-share context.
- Historical maize suitability classes from RES05-SIX.
- Historical maize attainable yield from RES05-YXX.
- Environmental and climate context.

Current task:
- Add historical continuous maize suitability at approximately 1 km from RES05-SXX30AS.

Later tasks, NOT part of this implementation:
- Future maize suitability classes.
- Future maize attainable yield.
- Future continuous maize suitability.
- Climate-model spread and agreement.
- Crop Water Indicators after dedicated metadata review.

Still outside scope:
- Actual harvested area.
- Actual maize production.
- Existing-farm yield gap.
- Other crops.
- Recommendations, crop selection, investment ranking, or intervention prioritization.

WHY THIS PRODUCT IS NEW
-----------------------
The existing Stage 03 package contains the following maize products:

1. RES05-SIX
   - Historical maize suitability classes.
   - Categorical values such as Very high, High, Moderate, Marginal, and Not suitable.
   - Approximate native resolution: 10 km.

2. RES05-YXX
   - Historical agro-ecological attainable maize yield.
   - Continuous values in kg dry weight per hectare.
   - Approximate native resolution: 10 km.

The new product is different:

3. RES05-SXX30AS
   - Historical continuous maize suitability index.
   - Continuous range: 0 to 10000.
   - Approximate native resolution: 30 arc-seconds, approximately 1 km.
   - Unit: Index.
   - Source NoData: -9.

RES05-SXX30AS does not replace RES05-SIX or RES05-YXX. All three products are complementary and must remain available in the dashboard:
- RES05-SIX answers: What broad suitability class applies?
- RES05-SXX30AS answers: What is the more spatially detailed continuous suitability index?
- RES05-YXX answers: What attainable yield is represented by the source product?

AUTHORITATIVE PRODUCT METADATA
------------------------------
Dataset title:
Crop suitability, continuous index (Global - 20 years average - ~1 km) - GAEZ v5

Dataset ID for this project:
GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS

Official map code:
RES05-SXX30AS

Theme:
Suitability and Attainable Yield

Sub-theme:
Suitability Index

Variable:
Suitability index range (0-10000); all land in grid cell

Native spatial resolution:
Approximately 1 km

Unit:
Index

Valid conceptual range:
0 to 10000

NoData:
-9

Scale factor:
1.0

Crop:
Maize

Crop code:
MZE

Historical period:
2001-2020

Period code:
HP0120

Climate source:
AgERA5

Climate scenario:
HIST

Management combinations:
- HRLM: Rainfed, high-input management.
- LRLM: Rainfed, low-input management.
- HILM: Irrigated, high-input management.
- LILM: Irrigated, low-input management.

Developers and lineage:
FAO and IIASA, derived from the GAEZ v5 Agro-Ecological Zones modelling framework.

IMPORTANT INTERPRETATION LIMITATION
-----------------------------------
The official metadata states that irrigated results are limited to grid cells where existing cropland is indicated in the GAEZ v5 land-cover inventory for 2020.

Parcel A is currently not agricultural land. Therefore:
- HRLM and LRLM are the principal continuous-suitability layers for evaluating the land's rainfed maize potential.
- HILM and LILM must still be inventoried and tested for completeness.
- Missing, masked, zero-coverage, or limited HILM/LILM results must NOT be interpreted as evidence that irrigation development has no potential.
- The dashboard must display the cropland-2020 limitation prominently for HILM and LILM.
- This product alone cannot determine irrigation feasibility. Water availability, water access, infrastructure, salinity, drainage, terrain, and crop-water requirements require separate evidence.

The existing required GAEZ core remains 16/16. The new Stage 03.1 report must distinguish:
- Original required GAEZ core: 16/16 verified.
- New supplemental RES05-SXX30AS analytical assets: 4/4 expected and independently verified.

Do not misleadingly change the previous result to 20/20 without clearly separating the original core and supplemental extension.

- readme_url: exact official README URL above
- dimensions_json_url: exact official filter JSON URL above
- sld_url: exact official SLD URL above
- limitation: irrigated outputs are limited to cells identified as existing cropland in GAEZ 2020 land cover

Recommended analytical asset keys:
- GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HRLM
- GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LRLM
- GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HILM
- GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LILM

For every asset, record:
- Exact TIFF URL.
- Exact JSON URL.
- Management code and full caption.
- Crop, period, climate source, and scenario.
- Expected unit, scale, NoData, and valid range.
- Verification status.
- HTTP response metadata where available.
- Source raster CRS, affine transform, dimensions, pixel size, mask representation, data type, checksum if the remote service exposes one, and retrieval timestamp.
- AOI intersection and valid-data evidence.
- Local clip path and clip checksum.

SUPPLEMENTAL VERIFICATION WORKFLOW
----------------------------------
Verification must operate on bounded remote raster windows. Do not download the full global GeoTIFF unless the existing verified method requires it and resource controls explicitly permit it.

For each of the four new GeoTIFFs:
1. Send a bounded availability request to the exact URL.
2. Open the remote Cloud Optimized GeoTIFF or range-readable GeoTIFF using the project's verified GDAL/rasterio method.
3. Read source metadata.
4. Confirm the raster overlaps Parcel A.
5. Transform Parcel A into the source CRS.
6. Read only the native source window intersecting Parcel A, including the project's documented all-touched handling where appropriate.
7. Preserve the native grid, native pixel size, and source mask.
8. Treat -9 as source NoData, while also respecting any internal validity mask.
9. Confirm that valid values, when present, are within 0-10000.
10. Save a georeferenced native-grid clip.
11. Reopen the clip and confirm CRS, transform, bounds, dimensions, data type, values, and mask.
12. Record valid native-cell count, intersecting native-cell count, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and QA status.
13. Calculate and store SHA-256 for the local clip and downloaded JSON sidecar.
14. Record failure honestly. Do not substitute zero, another mapset, WMTS colours, or a synthetic raster.

Verification statuses should include:
- VERIFIED_INSIDE_AOI
- VERIFIED_BUT_EMPTY_INSIDE_AOI
- NO_COVERAGE
- FAILED_REMOTE_ACCESS
- FAILED_METADATA_VALIDATION
- FAILED_RANGE_VALIDATION
- FAILED_CLIP_VALIDATION

HRLM and LRLM are selected required layers for Stage 03.1.
HILM and LILM are selected conditional layers because of the official 2020 cropland limitation. A technically valid but empty irrigated layer should not cause the rainfed Stage 03.1 extension to fail, provided the limitation is clearly documented and all required rainfed layers pass.

LOCAL OUTPUT NAMES
------------------
Use stable local names:
- clipped_data/rasters/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HRLM.tif
- clipped_data/rasters/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LRLM.tif
- clipped_data/rasters/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HILM.tif
- clipped_data/rasters/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LILM.tif

Retain JSON sidecars under a clear metadata directory, for example:
- metadata/source_metadata/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HRLM.json
- metadata/source_metadata/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LRLM.json
- metadata/source_metadata/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HILM.json
- metadata/source_metadata/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LILM.json

Do not package the full global TIFFs. Package only AOI clips, sidecars, metadata, visualizations, provenance, and QA evidence.

STATISTICS
----------
Treat RES05-SXX30AS as a continuous index, not a categorical raster.

For every layer with valid values, calculate:
- Minimum.
- Maximum.
- Area-weighted mean.
- Area-weighted population standard deviation.
- Weighted median.
- Weighted 5th percentile.
- Weighted 25th percentile.
- Weighted 75th percentile.
- Weighted 95th percentile.
- Intersecting native-cell count.
- Valid native-cell count.
- Valid area in hectares.
- Valid area percentage of the whole AOI.
- Masked/NoData area.
- Outside-footprint area.
- Quality flag.

Use the existing Stage 03 equal-area or geodesic area-accounting method. Do not create a new inconsistent area method.

Verify area closure:
valid area + masked area + outside-footprint area = AOI area within the configured tolerance.

Do not average categorical RES05-SIX class codes. Do not calculate production. Do not multiply the continuous suitability index by Parcel A area.

If too few native cells are available, show the individual native values and intersection areas and issue a FEW_NATIVE_CELLS quality flag. Never imply that increased display resolution creates field-scale evidence.

------------------------
Create four new static maps and four interactive dashboard layers.

Required static maps:
- maps/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HRLM.png
- maps/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LRLM.png
- maps/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__HILM.png, if valid data exist
- maps/GAEZ_V5_RES05_SXX30AS__LILM.png, if valid data exist

Map titles:
- Continuous maize suitability index - Rainfed high-input - 2001-2020
- Continuous maize suitability index - Rainfed low-input - 2001-2020
- Continuous maize suitability index - Irrigated high-input - 2001-2020
- Continuous maize suitability index - Irrigated low-input - 2001-2020

Legend requirements:
- Legend title: Continuous maize suitability index (0-10000)
- Unit description: Unitless GAEZ suitability index
- NoData: -9, transparent
- Valid source range: 0-10000
- Use one shared display domain for all four management layers so visual comparison is valid.
- Preferred default display domain: the official full range 0-10000.
- Parse and retain the official SLD. If the implementation uses a custom accessible continuous palette such as Viridis, identify it explicitly as a project display palette, not an official FAO palette.
- Display true minimum and maximum in the metadata panel even if the full official domain is used.
- Do not invent categorical names or thresholds for the continuous index.
- Do not automatically reclassify SXX30AS into the RES05-SIX classes unless official documentation provides an exact, validated conversion rule. The existing RES05-SIX product remains the authoritative categorical layer.

Every static map must include:
- Parcel A boundary.
- Clear title.
- Complete legend.
- Scale bar.
- North arrow or north indicator.
- Source note.
- Period.
- Management condition.
- Approximate native resolution.
- GAEZ/FAO/IIASA attribution.
- Visible interpretation note stating that approximately 1 km source cells are contextual and not field measurements.

DASHBOARD INTEGRATION
---------------------
Do not create a separate disconnected dashboard. Extend the existing standalone offline dashboard.

Add a new subgroup within the maize section:
Historical continuous maize suitability (~1 km)

Add four layer entries:
- Continuous suitability - HRLM
- Continuous suitability - LRLM
- Continuous suitability - HILM
- Continuous suitability - LILM

Required behaviour:
- Link every layer to its legend, metadata, table row, chart, and download by stable layer_id.
- Use the existing management selector where possible.
- Allow side-by-side comparison of HRLM versus LRLM.
- Allow comparison of RES05-SIX class and RES05-SXX30AS continuous index without suggesting that they are identical products.
- Keep the AOI boundary visible.
- Preserve opacity controls, zoom-to-AOI, scale, coordinates, grouped controls, metadata panel, downloads, and offline operation.
- Do not turn all four layers on by default. HRLM may be the default continuous layer; the others should remain readily selectable.
- HILM and LILM must display the cropland-2020 limitation in the visible metadata panel.

Add a short explanatory dashboard note:
RES05-SIX provides broad suitability classes at approximately 10 km, while RES05-SXX30AS provides a continuous suitability index at approximately 1 km. The products are complementary. The higher-resolution product does not constitute field-scale validation.

For HILM and LILM add:
Irrigated continuous-suitability results are limited by the GAEZ 2020 existing-cropland mask. Missing or limited coverage over Parcel A does not demonstrate that irrigation development is infeasible.

Create both CSV and JSON outputs containing the four new analytical assets and four supporting JSON objects.

---------------
Add automated tests for:
- Exact presence of four expected analytical asset keys.
- Exact presence and accessibility of four TIFF URLs and four JSON URLs.

- Unit equals Index.
- Scale factor is applied exactly once.
- Source NoData -9 is correctly masked.
- Valid zero values remain valid.

- Area closure passes tolerance.
- Shared legend domain is identical across all four layers.
- NoData is transparent.
- HILM and LILM carry the cropland-2020 warning.
- No categorical class captions are invented for SXX30AS.
- Existing 16 GAEZ layers and their checksums or analytical results remain unchanged unless a documented code-level bug correction requires otherwise.
- Every new dashboard layer has a working map, legend, metadata panel, table link, chart link where applicable, and download.
- Dashboard works from file:// with network disabled for packaged thematic content.
- No full global TIFF is included in the package.
- No previous Stage 03 output is silently removed.

RUN OUTCOME RULES
-----------------
Recommended Stage 03.1 acceptance logic:

COMPLETE:
- Original Stage 03 required outputs remain complete.
- Original GAEZ core remains 16/16 verified.
- HRLM and LRLM new analytical layers pass supplemental verification, extraction, statistics, visualization, metadata, and packaging.
- HILM and LILM either pass or are validly documented as conditionally empty/limited because of the source cropland mask.
- All required dashboard and QA links work.

COMPLETE_WITH_CONDITIONAL_IRRIGATED_GAPS:
- The original Stage 03 core passes.
- HRLM and LRLM pass.
- HILM and/or LILM are empty, masked, or limited for reasons consistent with the official cropland-2020 restriction.
- The condition is disclosed in maps, metadata, tables, QA, and run summary.

INCOMPLETE:
- HRLM or LRLM cannot be accessed, validated, clipped, interpreted, or packaged.
- Product metadata, unit, NoData, or grid cannot be resolved.
- Required dashboard integration or provenance is missing.
- Existing Stage 03 required functionality is lost or broken.

Recommended final package name:
stage03_1_historical_continuous_maize_package.zip

Preserve the previous package as an immutable baseline. Do not overwrite it.

Update the methodology and README to explain:
- Why RES05-SXX30AS was added.
- Difference among RES05-SIX, RES05-SXX30AS, and RES05-YXX.
- Exact product metadata.
- Historical scope only.
- Supplemental verification approach.
- Continuous statistics and legend.
- Approximate 1 km scale limitation.
- Irrigated-product cropland-2020 limitation.
- Why missing irrigated values cannot be interpreted as no irrigation potential.
- That future CMIP6 remains the next separate phase.

FINAL INSTRUCTION TO THE CODING AGENT
-------------------------------------
Implement this Stage 03.1 extension in the existing Parcel A repository and notebook.

Do not omit, shorten, rewrite, or remove existing Stage 03 functionality. Start from the current final Stage 03 code that produced the accepted stage03_inventory_package (1).zip. Preserve the original Stage 03 package as a baseline.

Add the four historical RES05-SXX30AS maize analytical GeoTIFFs and their four JSON sidecars using the exact URLs in this document. Perform bounded supplemental verification, clip to Parcel A on the native grid, calculate area-weighted continuous statistics, generate maps and a management comparison chart, update inventories and provenance, and integrate all valid outputs into the existing standalone offline dashboard.

Treat HRLM and LRLM as the required rainfed potential layers. Treat HILM and LILM as conditional because the official product limits irrigated results to cells identified as existing cropland in the GAEZ 2020 land-cover inventory.

Do not use WMTS colours or screenshots for statistics. Do not download or package unnecessary global rasters. Do not replace NoData with zero. Do not invent classes or convert the continuous index to RES05-SIX classes. Do not calculate production. Do not add future CMIP6 in this implementation. Do not add other crops. Do not create recommendations or rankings.

Rebuild the notebook's embedded bundle if applicable, verify its SHA-256, run all existing and new tests, execute Stage 03.1 against the same authoritative Stage 02 input package and AOI, and create one new portable results ZIP with an honest QA outcome.

END OF STAGE 03.1 IMPLEMENTATION GUIDE

================================================================================
STAGE 03.2 ADDENDUM
Add Confirmed Future Maize Suitability Classes to the Existing Workflow
================================================================================

PURPOSE AND RELATION TO STAGE 03.1
----------------------------------
This addendum extends the preceding Stage 03.1 guide. It does not replace any instruction above.

Implementation sequence:
1. Preserve the accepted Stage 03 baseline.
2. Implement and validate Stage 03.1 historical continuous maize suitability from RES05-SXX30AS.
3. After Stage 03.1 passes, implement this Stage 03.2 future maize suitability-class extension from RES05-SIX.

Do not combine unverified future attainable yield, future continuous suitability, climate-model agreement, or Crop Water Indicators into this implementation.

CONFIRMED FUTURE PRODUCT
------------------------
Dataset title:
Crop suitability, class index (Global - 20 years average - approximately 10 km) - GAEZ v5

Project dataset ID:
GAEZ_V5_RES05_SIX_FUTURE_MAIZE

Map code:
RES05-SIX

VERIFICATION AND EXTRACTION
---------------------------
Perform a separate supplemental verification for these four analytical TIFFs and four JSON sidecars. Do not rerun the full Stage 02 discovery process.

For each TIFF:
1. Validate exact filename dimensions.
2. Confirm remote access.
3. Read source CRS, transform, dimensions, pixel size, data type, NoData, and mask.
4. Confirm overlap with Parcel A.
5. Read only the necessary source window.
6. Preserve the native grid and use nearest-neighbour handling for categorical data.
7. Respect source NoData 0 and any internal mask.
8. Validate all observed valid codes against 1-9.
9. Block the layer if an unknown valid class code occurs.
10. Save a native-grid AOI clip.
11. Reopen and validate the clip.
12. Calculate local output SHA-256.
13. Record intersecting native-cell count, valid native-cell count, valid area, masked area, and outside-footprint area.
14. Preserve source JSON sidecars and their local checksums.

Do not use WMTS imagery, screenshots, display PNGs, or colour values for analytical statistics.

-------------------------
For each future layer:
- Calculate class area in hectares using the existing Stage 03 fractional native-cell intersection method.

- Report percentage of total AOI.
- Report valid, masked, and outside-footprint area separately.
- Report intersecting and valid native-cell counts.
- Retain the FEW_NATIVE_CELLS warning where applicable.
- Do not average class codes.
- Do not calculate production.
- Do not label class changes as benefits, losses, impacts, or recommendations.

HISTORICAL-FUTURE COMPARISON
----------------------------
Compare future layers only with the matching historical RES05-SIX management layer:
- Future HRLM versus historical HRLM.
- Future HILM versus historical HILM.

Required descriptive outputs:
- Historical and future maps with identical legends.
- Class-area comparison table.
- Class-transition area table only if the native grids align exactly or a documented source-grid overlay is correctly implemented.
- Dominant class for each source layer.
- Valid support and native-cell counts for every comparison.

If grids differ, do not perform pixel-to-pixel transition analysis until a scientifically defensible comparison method is documented. Never use display resampling to create analytical class transitions.

Do not use the words improved, worsened, benefit, damage, gain, loss, optimal, recommended, or preferred in generated conclusions. Use neutral descriptions such as:
- class distribution differs
- dominant class changes
- percentage of valid area in class X
- source ensemble represents the selected SSP and period

CONFIRMED PRODUCT IDENTITY
--------------------------
Dataset title:
Agro-ecological attainable yield, average of best occurring suitability class (Global - 20 years average - approximately 10 km) - GAEZ v5

Project dataset ID:
GAEZ_V5_RES05_YXX_FUTURE_MAIZE

Map code:
RES05-YXX

Theme:
Suitability and Attainable Yield

Sub-theme:
Agro-ecological Attainable Yield

Variable:
Average attainable yield of best occurring suitability class in grid cell

Data type:
Continuous raster

Approximate native resolution:
10 km

Unit for maize:
Kg (DW)/ha

Scale factor:
1.0

-9

Crop:
Maize

MZE

CMIP6 IPCC AR6

FP2140, corresponding to 2021-2040

ENSEMBLE

- SSP126, corresponding to SSP1-2.6

- SSP585, corresponding to SSP5-8.5

- HRLM: Rainfed, high-input management
- HILM: Irrigated, high-input management

The supplied future listings did not show LRLM or LILM. Do not invent those assets or treat their absence as a failed extraction unless authoritative product documentation explicitly says that they must exist for the selected future combination.

This product is not actual yield, observed farm yield, production, existing-farm yield gap, or a parcel-scale forecast. It is the GAEZ source-defined agro-ecological attainable yield metric.

SUPPLEMENTAL VERIFICATION AND EXTRACTION
----------------------------------------
Do not rerun Stage 01 or the complete Stage 02 discovery workflow. Add a distinct Stage 03.3 supplemental verification record.

For each of the six GeoTIFFs:
1. Confirm exact URL access and filename tokens.
2. Read source metadata with the existing verified GDAL/rasterio method.
3. Confirm the source unit, NoData, scale, CRS, transform, grid size, pixel dimensions, mask, and AOI overlap.
4. Transform Parcel A into the source CRS.
5. Read only the bounded native raster window required for Parcel A.
6. Preserve raw source values, native grid, and source mask.
7. Treat -9 as NoData while respecting any internal validity mask.
8. Confirm valid values are finite and non-negative unless authoritative metadata explicitly permits another valid range.
9. Save a georeferenced native-grid AOI clip.
10. Reopen and validate the saved clip.
11. Calculate a local SHA-256 checksum.
12. Download and retain the matching JSON sidecar and checksum.
13. Record valid native-cell count, intersecting native-cell count, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and quality flags.
14. Record failures without substituting another SSP, management condition, product, or synthetic value.

Do not package global source TIFFs.

STATISTICS
----------
Treat the product as a continuous yield-density raster.

For each valid layer calculate:
- minimum
- maximum
- area-weighted mean
- area-weighted population standard deviation
- weighted median
- weighted 5th percentile
- weighted 25th percentile
- weighted 75th percentile
- weighted 95th percentile
- intersecting native-cell count
- valid native-cell count
- valid area in hectares
- valid area percentage of total AOI
- masked area
- outside-footprint area
- quality flag

Use the existing equal-area or geodesic fractional native-cell accounting method. Verify area closure using the existing tolerance.

Keep values in Kg (DW)/ha. Do not silently convert to tonnes per hectare or fresh weight. If an optional display conversion to t DW/ha is added, retain the original values, explicitly use 1000 kg per tonne, label the converted unit as t DW/ha, and document the conversion once.

Do not convert dry weight to fresh weight without a verified maize-specific coefficient from authoritative documentation and an explicit future scope decision.

Do not multiply yield by Parcel A area. Do not calculate potential production, actual production, yield gap, revenue, or investment return.

VERIFICATION AND EXTRACTION
---------------------------
Do not rerun Stage 01 or the full Stage 02 discovery workflow. Add a separate Stage 03.5 supplemental verification report.

For each of the four TIFFs:
1. Confirm direct access to the exact public URL.
2. Confirm that the returned object matches the exact filename.
3. Read source CRS, transform, dimensions, pixel size, data type, NoData, and mask.
4. Confirm the source overlaps Parcel A.
5. Transform Parcel A into the source CRS.
6. Read only the bounded native-grid window intersecting Parcel A.
7. Preserve the native grid and source mask.
8. Use nearest-neighbour handling for categorical display or any required categorical grid operation.
9. Respect class 0 as source NoData and any internal validity mask.
10. Validate every observed valid code against classes 1 through 9.
11. Block any layer containing an unknown valid class code.
12. Save a native-grid AOI clip.
13. Reopen and validate CRS, transform, bounds, dimensions, data type, values, and mask.
14. Calculate SHA-256 for the local clip and retained JSON sidecar.
15. Record intersecting native-cell count, valid native-cell count, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and quality flags.
16. Record failures honestly without substituting ENSEMBLE, another GCM, another SSP, another period, another product, or synthetic values.

Do not use WMTS tiles, screenshots, rendered colours, or console previews for analytical calculations.

FIVE-GCM VERIFICATION AND EXTRACTION
------------------------------------
Treat Stage 03.5 and Stage 03.6 as one bounded five-GCM verification unit. Do not rerun Stage 01 or the full Stage 02 discovery workflow.

For every one of the ten TIFFs:
1. Confirm direct public access to the exact stable object URL.
2. Confirm the returned object corresponds to the exact expected filename.
3. Read source CRS, transform, dimensions, pixel size, data type, NoData, and internal mask.
4. Confirm overlap with Parcel A.
5. Transform Parcel A into the source CRS.
6. Read only the bounded native-grid window intersecting Parcel A.
7. Preserve the native grid, source values, and mask.
8. Use nearest-neighbour handling for categorical display or any necessary categorical grid operation.
9. Respect class 0 as NoData and respect the internal validity mask.
10. Validate every observed valid class against 1 through 9.
11. Block any layer containing an unknown valid class code.
12. Save a georeferenced native-grid AOI clip.
13. Reopen and validate CRS, transform, bounds, dimensions, resolution, data type, values, and mask.
14. Calculate SHA-256 for the local clip and retained JSON sidecar.
15. Record intersecting native-cell count, valid native-cell count, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and quality flags.
16. Compare source grids across the five models for each management condition.
17. Identify exact common valid support across all five models separately for HRLM and HILM.
18. Record failures without replacing a model with ENSEMBLE or another GCM.

Do not use WMTS tiles, screenshots, display PNGs, rendered colours, or console previews for analysis.

PER-MODEL CATEGORICAL STATISTICS
--------------------------------
For every valid model-management layer:
- Calculate class area in hectares using the existing Stage 03 fractional native-cell intersection method.
- Report percentage of valid area.
- Report percentage of total AOI.
- Report valid, masked, and outside-footprint area separately.
- Report intersecting and valid native-cell counts.
- Retain FEW_NATIVE_CELLS where applicable.
- Verify area closure within the existing tolerance.
- Keep HRLM and HILM separate.
- Do not average class codes.
- Do not calculate production.

FULL FIVE-MODEL AGREEMENT ANALYSIS FOR THE BOUNDED SCOPE
--------------------------------------------------------
Run this analysis separately for HRLM and HILM and only on exact common valid support across all five validated model layers.

Required categorical agreement outputs:
- Number of models assigning each valid class at each common-support analytical unit.
- Modal class only when a unique mode exists.
- Tie flag when no unique modal class exists.
- Exact unanimity indicator when all 5 models assign the same class.
- Agreement-count indicator for the modal class: 5 of 5, 4 of 5, or 3 of 5.
- Areas and percentages for 5-of-5, 4-of-5, 3-of-5, and tied outcomes.
- Per-class unanimity area and percentage.
- Per-class modal area and percentage, with agreement count retained.
- Five-model class-frequency vector for every common-support analytical unit.
- Pairwise same-class agreement area and percentage for every model pair.
- A five-model class cross-summary at AOI level.
- Common valid-support area, excluded non-common area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and native-cell counts.

Required minimum strong-agreement definition for project display:
- Strong agreement: at least 4 of 5 models assign the same class.
- Full agreement: 5 of 5 models assign the same class.
- Majority agreement: exactly 3 of 5 models assign the modal class with no tie.
- Tie or dispersed outcome: no unique class receives at least 3 model assignments.

These are project analytical labels for the five-model categorical comparison. They must not be presented as official GAEZ confidence categories, probabilities, or uncertainty classes.

Do not calculate arithmetic mean, median, standard deviation, minimum, maximum, or numeric range from class codes. Do not derive suitability gain or loss by subtracting class codes.

CLIMATE-MODEL SPREAD FOR A CATEGORICAL PRODUCT
----------------------------------------------
For RES05-SIX, represent spread categorically rather than numerically.

Required spread descriptors:
- Number of unique valid classes assigned across the five models.
- Most frequent class and its frequency.
- Presence or absence of a unique modal class.
- Lowest and highest suitability category only as ordered category labels, not as a numeric interval or arithmetic range.
- Count of adjacent versus non-adjacent category disagreements, if an ordered-category comparison is implemented and documented.
- Class-frequency entropy may be added only if clearly defined as a project-derived diversity index, calculated on model class frequencies, and never labelled as official GAEZ uncertainty.

Do not call class-code maximum minus minimum a suitability spread. Do not average or standardize class codes.

ENSEMBLE COMPARISON LIMITATION
------------------------------
The existing FP2140 ENSEMBLE SSP126 layer may be displayed alongside the five individual GCMs, but do not assume that ENSEMBLE equals:
- the modal class;
- a majority vote;
- a mean;
- a median;
- a probability-weighted result;
- the five-model agreement layer.

Compare ENSEMBLE with the individual-model-derived modal class only as a descriptive diagnostic unless the official README explicitly defines the ENSEMBLE aggregation method. Report match and mismatch areas without claiming which representation is more correct.

HISTORICAL COMPARISON LIMITATION
--------------------------------
If historical RES05-SIX is compared with future individual-model results:
- Compare HRLM with historical HRLM only.
- Compare HILM with historical HILM only.
- Use common valid support.
- Report categorical transitions and class distributions.
- Do not average class codes.
- Do not use causal or decision language.
- Do not label changes as improvement, deterioration, benefit, damage, gain, loss, optimal, recommended, or preferred.

12. Validate the reopened clip.
13. Calculate SHA-256 for the local clip and retained JSON sidecar.

17. Record failures without substituting ENSEMBLE, another model, another SSP, another period, another product, or synthetic data.

--------------------------

- 1: Very high; SI greater than 85; #1A9850.
- 2: High; SI greater than 70; #66BD63.
- 3: Good; SI greater than 55; #A6D96A.
- 4: Medium; SI greater than 40; #D9EF8B.
- 5: Moderate; SI greater than 25; #FEE08B.
- 6: Marginal; SI greater than 10; #FDAE61.
- 7: Very marginal; SI greater than 0; #F46D43.

7. Treat class 0 as NoData and preserve valid classes 1 through 9.
8. Block unknown valid class codes.
9. Use nearest-neighbour handling for categorical display or necessary categorical grid operations.
10. Save and reopen the georeferenced native-grid AOI clip.
11. Validate the reopened clip.
12. Calculate SHA-256 for each clip and retained JSON sidecar.
13. Record intersecting cells, valid cells, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and quality flags.
14. Reevaluate grid compatibility and exact common valid support across all five models separately for HRLM and HILM.
15. Record failures without substituting ENSEMBLE, duplicate GFDL-ESM4, another model, SSP, period, product, or synthetic data.

The supplied listing contains ten analytical GeoTIFFs and ten matching JSON sidecars. Listing completeness does not itself certify direct public access, valid raster metadata, class codes, AOI overlap, grid compatibility, common valid support, successful extraction, analytical completion, dashboard integration, or QA completion.

AUTHORITATIVE PRODUCT IDENTITY
------------------------------
Dataset title:
Crop suitability, class index (Global - 20 years average - approximately 10 km) - GAEZ v5
Project dataset ID:
GAEZ_V5_RES05_SIX_FIVE_GCM_FP6180_SSP585_MAIZE
Map code:
RES05-SIX
Theme:
Suitability and Attainable Yield
Sub-theme:
Suitability Class
Variable:
Crop suitability index in classes; all land in grid cell
Data type:
Categorical raster
Approximate native resolution:
10 km
Unit:
Class
Scale factor:
1.0
Source NoData:
0
Crop:
Maize
Crop code:
MZE
Confirmed period:
FP6180, 2061-2080
Confirmed SSP:
SSP585 / SSP5-8.5
Confirmed management codes:
- HRLM: Rainfed, high-input management.
- HILM: Irrigated, high-input management.
Confirmed individual climate models:
- GFDL-ESM4.
- IPSL-CM6A-LR.
- MPI-ESM1-2-HR.
- MRI-ESM2-0.
- UKESM1-0-LL.

AUTHORITATIVE CLASS LEGEND
--------------------------
- 1: Very high; SI greater than 85; #1A9850.
- 2: High; SI greater than 70; #66BD63.
- 3: Good; SI greater than 55; #A6D96A.
- 4: Medium; SI greater than 40; #D9EF8B.
- 5: Moderate; SI greater than 25; #FEE08B.
- 6: Marginal; SI greater than 10; #FDAE61.
- 7: Very marginal; SI greater than 0; #F46D43.
- 8: Not suitable; #D73027.
- 9: Water; #2C7BB6.
- 0: NoData; transparent.
Do not average, subtract, standardize, or calculate arithmetic dispersion from class codes. Class codes are categorical labels, not interval-scale measurements.

MANIFEST AND LAYER-CATALOGUE RECORDS
------------------------------------
Add ten FP6180 SSP585 analytical asset records and ten matching JSON references.
Recommended key template:
GAEZ_V5_RES05_SIX__FP6180__<NORMALIZED_GCM>__SSP585__MZE__<HRLM_OR_HILM>
For every record include dataset ID, map code, variable, crop and crop code, period code and dates, exact climate-model code, SSP and management codes, data type, unit, scale factor, source NoData and mask rule, shared legend ID, exact TIFF and JSON names and URLs, official-resource URLs, listing evidence, direct-access result, raster metadata, AOI overlap, common-support status, local paths, SHA-256, limitations, and verification status.

DIRECT ACCESS, VALIDATION, EXTRACTION, AND PER-MODEL STATISTICS
--------------------------------------------------------------
1. Confirm direct public access to each exact stable URL and exact returned filename.
2. Validate filename tokens for product, period, intended GCM, SSP, crop, and management.
3. Read and validate CRS, affine transform, dimensions, pixel size, data type, NoData, and internal mask.
4. Confirm Parcel A overlap and transform Parcel A into the source CRS.
5. Read only the bounded native-grid window intersecting Parcel A.
6. Preserve native grid, source values, and source mask.
7. Treat class 0 as NoData; preserve valid classes 1 through 9; block unknown valid codes.
8. Use nearest-neighbour handling for categorical display or necessary categorical grid operations.
9. Save and reopen each georeferenced native-grid AOI clip and validate it.
10. Calculate SHA-256 for every clip and JSON sidecar.
11. Record intersecting cells, valid cells, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and quality flags.
12. Calculate class area using the established fractional native-cell intersection method and report percentages of valid area and total AOI.
13. Retain FEW_NATIVE_CELLS where applicable and verify area closure within tolerance.
14. Keep HRLM and HILM separate.
15. Compare five model grids and determine exact common valid support separately for HRLM and HILM.
16. Record failures without substitution.
Do not use WMTS tiles, screenshots, previews, rendered colours, or console pages for analysis. Do not package global source TIFFs. Do not average class codes or calculate production.

FIVE-MODEL FP6180 SSP585 AGREEMENT AND CATEGORICAL SPREAD
---------------------------------------------------------
- unique modal class where present;
- tie flag without arbitrary tie breaking;
- 5-of-5 full agreement;
- 4-of-5 strong agreement;
- exactly 3-of-5 majority agreement with a unique mode;
- dispersed or tied outcomes;
- five-model class-frequency vectors;
- unique-class count as categorical spread;
- per-class unanimity and modal areas;
- all ten unique pairwise same-class comparisons;
- common-support denominator and excluded-area accounting.
These are project-derived categorical summaries, not official GAEZ probabilities, confidence classes, consensus products, or uncertainty classes.

COMPARATIVE ANALYSIS
--------------------
After applicable analytical validation, compare FP6180 SSP585 with matching periods and SSPs already verified. Compare the same GCM with the same GCM and HRLM with HRLM or HILM with HILM. Use documented common valid support. Compare class-area composition, categorical transitions, modal-class distributions where unique, agreement-category distributions, and categorical-spread distributions. Disclose denominators and excluded areas. Do not subtract class codes or use causal, ranking, recommendation, improvement, deterioration, benefit, damage, gain, or loss language.
ENSEMBLE layers remain separately labelled diagnostics. Do not infer that ENSEMBLE equals a modal class, vote, mean, median, probability, or project-derived agreement layer.

Extend the same standalone offline dashboard only after validation. Include period, SSP, five climate-model, management, individual-model, five-model-agreement, categorical-spread, pairwise-agreement, ENSEMBLE-diagnostic, cross-SSP, and cross-period controls as supported by verified data.

DIRECT ACCESS, VALIDATION, EXTRACTION, AND PER-MODEL STATISTICS
--------------------------------------------------------------
1. Confirm direct public access to each exact stable URL and exact returned filename.
2. Validate filename tokens for product, period, intended GCM, SSP, crop, and management.
3. Read and validate CRS, affine transform, dimensions, pixel size, data type, NoData, and internal mask.
4. Confirm Parcel A overlap and transform Parcel A into the source CRS.
5. Read only the bounded native-grid window intersecting Parcel A.
6. Preserve native grid, source values, and source mask.
7. Treat class 0 as NoData; preserve valid classes 1 through 9; block unknown valid codes.
8. Use nearest-neighbour handling for categorical display or necessary categorical grid operations.
9. Save and reopen each georeferenced native-grid AOI clip and validate it.
10. Calculate SHA-256 for every clip and JSON sidecar.
11. Record intersecting cells, valid cells, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and quality flags.
12. Calculate class area using the established fractional native-cell intersection method and report percentages of valid area and total AOI.
13. Retain FEW_NATIVE_CELLS where applicable and verify area closure within tolerance.
14. Keep HRLM and HILM separate.
15. Compare five model grids and determine exact common valid support separately for HRLM and HILM.
16. Record failures without substitution.
Do not use WMTS tiles, screenshots, previews, rendered colours, or console pages for analysis. Do not package global source TIFFs. Do not average class codes or calculate production.

--------------------------------------------------------------

16. Record failures without substitution.

DIRECT ACCESS, VALIDATION, EXTRACTION, AND PER-MODEL STATISTICS
--------------------------------------------------------------
1. Confirm direct public access to each exact stable URL and exact returned filename.
2. Validate filename tokens for product, period, intended GCM, SSP, crop, and management.
3. Read and validate CRS, affine transform, dimensions, pixel size, data type, NoData, and internal mask.
4. Confirm Parcel A overlap and transform Parcel A into the source CRS.
5. Read only the bounded native-grid window intersecting Parcel A.
6. Preserve native grid, source values, and source mask.
7. Treat class 0 as NoData; preserve valid classes 1 through 9; block unknown valid codes.
8. Use nearest-neighbour handling for categorical display or necessary categorical grid operations.
9. Save and reopen each georeferenced native-grid AOI clip and validate it.
10. Calculate SHA-256 for every clip and JSON sidecar.
11. Record intersecting cells, valid cells, valid area, masked area, outside-footprint area, and quality flags.
12. Calculate class area using the established fractional native-cell intersection method and report percentages of valid area and total AOI.
13. Retain FEW_NATIVE_CELLS where applicable and verify area closure within tolerance.
14. Keep HRLM and HILM separate.
15. Compare five model grids and determine exact common valid support separately for HRLM and HILM.
16. Record failures without substitution.
Do not use WMTS tiles, screenshots, previews, rendered colours, or console pages for analysis. Do not package global source TIFFs. Do not average class codes or calculate production.

- unique modal class where present;

- per-class unanimity and modal areas;

These are project-derived categorical summaries, not official GAEZ probabilities, confidence classes, consensus products, or uncertainty classes.

Unlike RES05-SIX class codes, RES05-YXX values are continuous yield-density values, so arithmetic summaries are allowed after unit, scale, mask, and common-support validation. These summaries are project-derived and are not official GAEZ probabilities, confidence intervals, consensus products, or uncertainty classes. Do not call the five-model mean or median an official ensemble.
